    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a serpent so vast that even those who drink amṛta daily are still afraid — the demigods' immortality does not free them from fear, because fear is not removed by living long but by taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa. The boys playing tag in the previous verses have no nectar and no fear; the devas have nectar and are terrified.

    Stories
    Gajendra crying out from the lake

    Gajendra, king of elephants (SB 8.2–3), was held by a crocodile for a thousand celestial years. None of his elephant strength, none of the demigods, could free him. Only when he abandoned all hope in his own power and called Bhagavān's name did the Lord arrive on Garuḍa.

    Aghāsura is the cosmic crocodile of this chapter. The devas fear him from behind clouds; only Kṛṣṇa's direct intervention removes the threat. Gauḍīyas read both episodes as instances of śaraṇāgati — the rakṣiṣyatīti viśvāsa limb: 'He will surely protect.'

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.14

    ద‍ృష్ట్వార్భకాన్ కృష్ణముఖానఘాసుర: కంసానుశిష్ట: స బకీబకానుజ: । అయం తు మే సోదరనాశకృత్తయో- ర్ద్వయోర్మమైనం సబలం హనిష్యే ॥ ౧౪ ॥

    Important Words
    • kaṁsa-anuśiṣṭaḥ — instructed by Kaṁsa
      Compound: Kaṁsa (the demoniac king) + anuśiṣṭa (instructed). The Vraja-līlā has a political subplot — Kaṁsa is the cause of every demon attack. Gauḍīyas note: Kaṁsa's enmity functions as inverted bhakti (vairānubandha); he attains liberation by death at Kṛṣṇa's hands.
    • bakī-baka-anujaḥ — younger brother of Pūtanā and Baka
      Compound: bakī (the female demon, i.e. Pūtanā) + baka (the heron demon) + anuja (younger brother). The three asuras are siblings — Pūtanā 10.6, Bakāsura 10.11, Aghāsura 10.12. Vraja-līlā has a family of demons being progressively absorbed.
    • sodara-nāśa-kṛt — killer of my siblings
      Compound: sodara (uterine sibling) + nāśa (destruction) + kṛt (doer). Demoniac logic: my family is killed, therefore the killer must be killed. The very logic that brings him to Kṛṣṇa is what consumes him.
    • haniṣye — I shall kill
      Future of han- (to strike, kill). The same root as in Bhagavad-gītā 2.19's na hanti na hanyate — 'one neither kills nor is killed.' Aghāsura's intent to kill the Unkillable becomes the comedy of the chapter.
    Aghāsura, who had been sent by Kaṁsa, was the younger brother of Pūtanā and Bakāsura. Therefore when he came and saw Kṛṣṇa at the head of all the cowherd boys, he thought, “This Kṛṣṇa has killed my sister and brother, Pūtanā and Bakāsura. Therefore, in order to please them both, I shall kill this Kṛṣṇa, along with His assistants, the other cowherd boys.”
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Aghāsura, who had been sent by Kaṁsa, was the younger brother of Pūtanā and Bakāsura. Therefore when he came and saw Kṛṣṇa at the head of all the cowherd boys, he thought, "This Kṛṣṇa has killed my sister and brother, Pūtanā and Bakāsura. Therefore, in order to please them both, I shall kill this Kṛṣṇa, along with His assistants, the other cowherd boys."
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because they killed my brother and sister, I will kill them. Instead of mama sometimes atha is seen. The version with mama is accepted by all including Citsukha. Using atha avoids repeating the word “my” twice (me and mama). Kṛṣṇa was the killer of my brother and sister, of those two. Therefore I will kill him. Or, Kṛṣṇa was the killer of my brother and sister. To show enmity on their behalf (tayoḥ dvayoḥ) I will kill him.

    • SNS SB 10-12-14 - Vaishnava-Etikette-5 - 1990-06-07 Berlin DE
      His Holiness Sacinandana Swami· 1990-06-07· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a younger brother sworn to avenge two older siblings already killed in battle — except the 'older siblings' are the witch Pūtanā and the heron Bakāsura, and the boy he means to kill is the Supreme Person Himself. Vendetta misunderstood as strategy: Aghāsura thinks he is the third arrow; he is actually the third gift.

    Stories
    Pūtanā receiving the place of a nurse

    Pūtanā came to murder the infant Kṛṣṇa by poisoning her own breasts (SB 10.6). Yet because she had put on a mother's form and offered her breast — even with murderous intent — Kṛṣṇa accepted her as a nursemaid and gave her a position in His eternal līlā (SB 10.6.35).

    Gauḍīyas read this as the principle that operates again here for Agha: contact with Kṛṣṇa, even hostile contact, purifies. Aghāsura comes to kill his sister's killer, and ends as the Lord's associate in verse 38.

    Important Words
    • tila-āpaḥ — sesame and water offering for the dead
      Compound: tila (sesame) + āpa (water). The traditional śrāddha offering. Aghāsura grotesquely re-imagines Kṛṣṇa and the boys as the funeral offering for his dead siblings — a black inversion of yajña.
    • naṣṭa-samāḥ — as good as dead
      Compound: naṣṭa (lost, destroyed) + sama (equivalent). The Vrajavāsīs, if separated from Kṛṣṇa, are 'equivalent to dead' — Gauḍīyas read this as the technical state of viraha (separation), which is itself a form of intensified bhakti.
    • vraja-okasaḥ — the residents of Vraja
      Compound: vraja + okas. Vraja-okasaḥ is the technical name for the residents whose entire prāṇa is Kṛṣṇa — Nanda, Yaśodā, the gopas, the gopīs. Their love is so total that physical death follows separation.
    • prāṇa-bhṛtaḥ — those who carry the life-air
      Compound: prāṇa (life-air) + bhṛt (bearer). The 'bearers' of prāṇa — i.e. the living — are those for whom Kṛṣṇa is the prāṇa-of-prāṇa. Gauḍīyas: Kṛṣṇa is prāṇasya-prāṇa (Bhagavad-gītā 7.10), the life of life itself.
    Aghāsura thought: If somehow or other I can make Kṛṣṇa and His associates serve as the last offering of sesame and water for the departed souls of my brother and sister, then the inhabitants of Vrajabhūmi, for whom these boys are the life and soul, will automatically die. If there is no life, there is no need for the body; consequently, when their sons are dead, naturally all the inhabitants of Vraja will die.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Aghāsura thought: If somehow or other I can make Kṛṣṇa and His associates serve as the last offering of sesame and water for the departed souls of my brother and sister, then the inhabitants of Vrajabhūmi, for whom these boys are the life and soul, will automatically die. If there is no life, there is no need for the body; consequently, when their sons are dead, naturally all the inhabitants of Vraja will die.
    Commentary

    Seeing Krsna and the others (krsna mukhan), Aghasura (sah), the younger brother of Putana (baki) and Bakasura, began to consider (vyavasya-- see verse 16. verse 14 second half and 15 are Aghasura’s thoughts), "This person (ayam tu) has killed my older sister Putana (baki) and my brother (baka) (sodara nasa krt). Therefore (atha) I will kill him on their behalf, as a pinda offering along with his followers (sabalam). If Krsna and the other boys become the offering to the pretas (tilapah) for my brother and sister (mat suhrdoh), Nanda and the other inhabitants of Vraja will naturally die. If the life has left the body do we have to worry about the bodies (varsmasu)? Those who live ( ye prana bhutah), live through their children (te prajasavah). Therefore if Krsna, their child, dies, they will die, without our having to kill them."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Naṣṭa-samāḥ means almost dead.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Imagine a thief reasoning that to kill a city he need only kill its children — because once the children are dead, the parents will follow from grief. Aghāsura has correctly diagnosed Vraja's heart: Kṛṣṇa and the boys are the prāṇa of Vrajavāsīs. He has misdiagnosed only one thing — that any death is possible here.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.16

    ↗
    ఇతి వ్యవస్యాజగరం బృహద్ వపు: స యోజనాయామమహాద్రిపీవరమ్ । ధృత్వాద్భ‍ుతం వ్యాత్తగుహాననం తదా పథి వ్యశేత గ్రసనాశయా ఖల: ॥ ౧౬ ॥
    iti vyavasyājagaraṁ bṛhad vapuḥ

    Important Words
    • ājagaram — a python form
      From ajagara (python). The python is the largest non-mythic Indian serpent, but Aghāsura's body is mountain-scale. Gauḍīyas note: the python in Bhāgavatam (e.g. Nahuṣa in 6.13, Sudarśana in 10.34) is a recurring shape for cursed or demonized beings.
    • yojana-āyāma — eight miles in length
      Compound: yojana (~ 8 miles) + āyāma (extension). The traditional cosmographic unit. Bhāgavatam is precise about size — the body of the demon is not vague, it is measurable. Kṛṣṇa's expansion in verse 30 will exceed it.
    • mahā-adri-pīvaram — as thick as a great mountain
      Compound: mahā (great) + adri (mountain) + pīvara (thick). The Vraja landscape has Govardhana, a real mountain, just to the south. Aghāsura mimics that scale — Vraja's true mountain is Govardhana, lifted in chapter 25; this is its parody.
    • guhā-ānanam — mouth like a cave
      Compound: guhā (cave) + ānana (mouth). The cave-mouth is what allows the cowherd boys to mistake the demon for forest scenery in the next verse. The līlā uses optical ambiguity — Kṛṣṇa's pastimes often run on what looks like one thing but is another.
    • khalaḥ — the wicked one
      khala — the morally crooked. Bhāgavatam uses khala specifically for demoniac beings whose envy is constitutional. Distinct from a person who happens to act badly; this is character, not behavior.
    After thus deciding, that crooked Aghāsura assumed the form of a huge python, as thick as a big mountain and as long as eight miles. Having assumed this wonderful python’s body, he spread his mouth like a big cave in the mountains and lay down on the road, expecting to swallow Kṛṣṇa and His associates the cowherd boys.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    After thus deciding, that crooked Aghāsura assumed the form of a huge python, as thick as a big mountain and as long as eight miles. Having assumed this wonderful python's body, he spread his mouth like a big cave in the mountains and lay down on the road, expecting to swallow Kṛṣṇa and His associates the cowherd boys.
    Commentary

    Thinking in this way, Aghasura lay on the path in order to devour Krsna. He was a yojana in length (eight miles). He was as fat (pivaram) as a huge mountain (mahadri). His mouth spread open (vyattam) like a cave.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Resolving (vyavsya) thus, he spread himself out without moving since he was a cheating killer (khalaḥ), inclined to swallow little boys.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Resolving (vyavsya) thus, at that moment (tadā), he spread himself out without moving since he was a cheating killer (khalaḥ), who could easily swallow them all by tricky means. He would swallow them all at once since he was a python. Or he was cruel, with a proclivity for eating such boys.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a hunter setting a trap so large that he himself disappears into it — a cave-mouth across the entire road, a serpent the length of eight miles. The geometry is unmistakable: only one road, only one way through, only one mouth. Aghāsura has made himself into the very door of his own death.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.17

    ↗
    ధరాధరోష్ఠో జలదోత్తరోష్ఠో దర్యాననాన్తో గిరిశ‍ృఙ్గదంష్ట్ర: । ధ్వాన్తాన్తరాస్యో వితతాధ్వజిహ్వ: పరుషానిలశ్వాసదవేక్షణోష్ణ: ॥ ౧౭ ॥
    dharādharoṣṭho jaladottaroṣṭho

    Important Words
    • dharā-adhara-oṣṭhaḥ — lower lip on the earth
      Compound: dharā (earth) + adhara (lower) + oṣṭha (lip). The demon's geometry literally spans the cosmography — earth to clouds. The whole verse is a deliberate cosmic anti-icon, contrasted later with Kṛṣṇa's universal form (chapter 14, when Brahmā sees Him as the source of all).
    • jalada-uttara-oṣṭhaḥ — upper lip touching the clouds
      Compound: jalada (cloud) + uttara (upper) + oṣṭha (lip). Cloud-touching is normally a divine attribute (Viṣṇu's Trivikrama form). Aghāsura misappropriates the imagery — he is a fake virāṭ-rūpa.
    • dhvānta-antaḥ-āsyaḥ — the inside of his mouth was completely dark
      Compound: dhvānta (darkness) + antaḥ (inside) + āsya (mouth). Inside the demon is tamas — the same tamas that demoniac nature lives in. Kṛṣṇa entering this darkness is the dīpa entering the well (Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān's presence cancels tamas instantly).
    • dava-īkṣaṇa-uṣṇaḥ — with eyes burning like a forest-fire
      Compound: dava (forest fire) + īkṣaṇa (gaze) + uṣṇa (hot). The forest-fire image will return in chapter 19 when Kṛṣṇa actually swallows a literal dāvāgni. Foreshadowing: the demon's fire is theatrical, Kṛṣṇa's appetite for fire is real.
    His lower lip rested on the surface of the earth, and his upper lip was touching the clouds in the sky. The borders of his mouth resembled the sides of a big cave in a mountain, and the middle of his mouth was as dark as possible. His tongue resembled a broad traffic-way, his breath was like a warm wind, and his eyes blazed like fire.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    His lower lip rested on the surface of the earth, and his upper lip was touching the clouds in the sky. The borders of his mouth resembled the sides of a big cave in a mountain, and the middle of his mouth was as dark as possible. His tongue resembled a broad traffic-way, his breath was like a warm wind, and his eyes blazed like fire.
    Commentary

    His lower lip touched the earth and his upper lip reached the clouds. The front of the mouth was like two mountain caves. The interior of the mouth was dark, and the tongue was like a wide road. His breath was like burning fire and his gaze was like a forest fire.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Instead of paruṣa sometimes parṣa is seen. He had hot nostrils breathing harsh wind and hot eyes blazing like fire. Or he was hot because of his harsh breath and fiery eyes.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a mouth so vast that the lower lip drags in the soil and the upper lip combs the clouds — eyes that burn like a forest fire, tongue stretched as a road. This is not just a big snake; it is the parody of a world. Aghāsura has tried to become a cosmos that swallows; in chapter 14 Kṛṣṇa will show Brahmā the real cosmos He contains.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.18

    ↗
    ద‍ృష్ట్వా తం తాద‍ృశం సర్వే మత్వా వృన్దావనశ్రియమ్ । వ్యాత్తాజగరతుణ్డేన హ్యుత్ప్రేక్షన్తే స్మ లీలయా ॥ ౧౮ ॥
    dṛṣṭvā taṁ tādṛśaṁ sarve

    Important Words
    • vṛndāvana-śriyam — the beauty of Vṛndāvana
      Compound: Vṛndāvana + śrī (beauty, splendor). The boys mistake the demon for vana-śobhā — they read everything in Vraja as beautiful. Gauḍīyas: this is the rasika eye, the antithesis of Aghāsura's envious one.
    • ajagara-tuṇḍena — by a python's mouth
      Compound: ajagara (python) + tuṇḍa (snout, beak, mouth). Even when they correctly read the form, they treat it as art — a model python — not a threat. Sakhya-rasa has no fear because its support is Kṛṣṇa.
    • utprekṣante — they imagine, they fancifully suppose
      From ut + prekṣ- (to look up, imagine). The technical term for poetic fancy. Śuka tells us the boys are doing poetry as they walk into the demon's mouth — the entire līlā runs at the level of alaṅkāra.
    • līlayā — as play
      From līl- (to play). Everything in Vraja is līlā. The boys treat the demon as part of their game — and from Kṛṣṇa's perspective, that is exactly what it is.
    Upon seeing this demon’s wonderful form, which resembled a great python, the boys thought that it must be a beautiful scenic spot of Vṛndāvana. Thereafter, they imagined it to be similar to the mouth of a great python. In other words, the boys, unafraid, thought that it was a statue made in the shape of a great python for the enjoyment of their pastimes.
    Purport

    Some of the boys, upon seeing this wonderful phenomenon, thought that it was in fact a python, and they were fleeing from the spot. But others said, “Why are you fleeing? It is not possible that a python like this is staying here. This is a spot of beauty for sporting.” This is what they imagined.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Upon seeing this demon's wonderful form, which resembled a great python, the boys thought that it must be a beautiful scenic spot of Vṛndāvana. Thereafter, they imagined it to be similar to the mouth of a great python. In other words, the boys, unafraid, thought that it was a statue made in the shape of a great python for the enjoyment of their pastimes.
    Commentary

    Some of the boys on seeing Aghasura started to run away, thinking it was a big snake. Other boys however considered that Aghasura was one of the wonders of Vrndavana. They comforted their fearful friends by saying, "Oh fools, a snake can never be this big. It is something created by the Lord out of clay so that we can appreciate the splendor of Vrndavana even more. He has made it in the form of a huge snake with a big gaping snout." In this way they thought of the Lord’s creation as something like the gaping mouth of the serpent (upreksyanta), without fear (lilaya).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The word hi indicates certainty. They compared it to snake as a jest.

    Related values

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture children walking into what they think is a stage-prop cave at a temple festival — painted lips, painted teeth, all part of the décor. Their unawareness is not stupidity; it is the structural sweetness of Vraja, where nothing can actually harm them and everything is interpreted as Kṛṣṇa's play. The cowherd boys' fearlessness is the proof of where they are.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.19

    ↗
    అహో మిత్రాణి గదత సత్త్వకూటం పుర: స్థితమ్ । అస్మత్సఙ్గ్రసనవ్యాత్తవ్యాలతుణ్డాయతే న వా ॥ ౧౯ ॥

    Important Words
    • mitrāṇi — O friends
      Vocative plural of mitra (friend). The keynote address of sakhya-rasa. The boys speak to each other as friends, with Kṛṣṇa among them as one of the mitras — not as Lord-and-servant in this moment, but as peer-among-peers.
    • sattva-kūṭam — a living creature in disguise
      Compound: sattva (living being) + kūṭa (cheat, deception). 'A creature pretending to be a hill.' The boys correctly diagnose: this is a creature disguised. The Gauḍīya lesson — māyā always wears scenery.
    • saṅgrasana — to devour all of us together
      From sam + gras- (to swallow whole). The boys see the threat clearly and walk in anyway. Not naïve confidence — confidence in Kṛṣṇa, even when they cannot name what it is they are confident in.
    The boys said: Dear friends, is this creature dead, or is it actually a living python with its mouth spread wide just to swallow us all? Kindly clear up this doubt.
    Purport

    The friends began to discuss among themselves the reality of the wonderful creature laying before them. Was it dead, or was it actually a living python trying to swallow them up?

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The boys said: Dear friends, is this creature dead, or is it actually a living python with its mouth spread wide just to swallow us all? Kindly clear up this doubt.
    Commentary

    Some called out to the chief boys to confirm their thoughts. "Is this some type of immovable living entity?" Kuta means a peak, indicating some large animal. "Is it some peaceful tiger or other big animal, who has his mouth open as if to devour us?"

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Please tell us whether this is some immobile creature (sattva-kūṭam) in front of us, whose front part is in the form of a snake’s mouth gaping in order to devour us.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Imagine children debating whether a sleeping crocodile on the riverbank is real — leaning in, poking, comparing notes. The danger they are debating is fatal; their composure is total. This is what living in Kṛṣṇa's company does: it removes the existential weight from what would otherwise terrify.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.20

    ↗
    సత్యమర్కకరారక్తముత్తరాహనువద్ ధనమ్ । అధరాహనువద్రోధస్తత్ప్రతిచ్ఛాయయారుణమ్ ॥ ౨౦ ॥
    satyam arka-karāraktam
    [satyam arka-karaaraktam]
    uttarā-hanuvad ghanam
    [uttaraa-hanuvad ghanam]
    adharā-hanuvad rodhas
    [adharaa-hanuvad rodhas]
    tat-praticchāyayāruṇam
    [tat-pratichchaayayaarunam]
    Word-for-word
    satyam — now the boys decided that it was in fact a living python arka - kara - āraktam — appearing like the sunshine uttarā - hanuvat ghanam — on the cloud resembling the upper lips adharā - hanuvat — resembling the lower lips rodhaḥ — big bank tat - praticchāyayā — by the reflection of sunshine aruṇam — reddish .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)

    Important Words
    • arka-kara-āraktam — reddened by the sun's rays
      Compound: arka (sun) + kara (ray) + ārakta (reddened). The upper lip is sun-touched cloud-color. Bhāgavatam's nature-imagery is precise: pinks, reds, the colors of dawn — all the colors of Vraja's sky are catalogued in the demon's body too.
    • uttarā-hanuvat — like the upper jaw
      Compound: uttara (upper) + hanu (jaw) + vat (like). Anatomical precision plus simile-suffix. The boys, even in their playful interpretation, see clearly — Vraja-rasa is not blurry; it is poetic comparison.
    • adharā-hanuvat — like the lower jaw
      Compound: adhara (lower) + hanu (jaw) + vat (like). The pair uttarā/adharā creates a verse-internal symmetry — sky-and-shadow, upper-and-lower. Aghāsura is symmetrically described because Kṛṣṇa's līlā has aesthetic structure even in its parodies.
    • tat-praticchāyayā — by its reflection
      Compound: tat (that) + praticchāyā (reflection). The 'reflection' of the cloud — a doubled image. Gauḍīyas note the recurrence of pratibhāsa / praticchāyā imagery in Bhāgavatam: māyā is always a reflection of the real.
    Thereafter they decided: Dear friends, this is certainly an animal sitting here to swallow us all. Its upper lip resembles a cloud reddened by the sunshine, and its lower lip resembles the reddish shadows of a cloud.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Thereafter they decided: Dear friends, this is certainly an animal sitting here to swallow us all. Its upper lip resembles a cloud reddened by the sunshine, and its lower lip resembles the reddish shadows of a cloud.
    Commentary

    One boy agreed with them. "" The cloud reddened by the sun seems to be his upper lip, and its reflection reddening the ground seems to be his lower lip." Hanu here means lip or jaw rather than chin since there is no upper and lower chin.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The mountain slope made red by the reflection from the cloud is like its lower lip. Some boys asked the question in the previous verse. Other boys answer in four verses, agreeing with them. You speak the truth (satyam). It is red with the sunlight. This indicates that the boys were going west, and the demon was facing east. Thus the morning light lit up the snake. Uttarā in the feminine instead of masculine is poetic license.

    Satya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture children matching cloud-colors to a creature's lips — pink and red, sun and shadow. Their analysis is correct in every detail; only the conclusion they should draw, they will not. Vraja teaches: accurate perception of danger is compatible with absolute fearlessness, when Kṛṣṇa is the company.

    ↗

    Important Words
    • naga-udare — in the belly of a mountain
      Compound: naga (mountain) + udara (belly). The boys read the demon's body as cave-geography — mountain bellies, mountain peaks. The poet is laying down imagery that will be inverted in chapter 25 when Kṛṣṇa lifts an actual mountain.
    • tuṅga-śṛṅga-ālayaḥ — abode of high peaks
      Compound: tuṅga (lofty) + śṛṅga (peak) + ālaya (abode). 'Dwelling-place of high peaks' for what are actually fangs. Gauḍīya āyurvedic note: daṁṣṭra (fang) carries venom — but here the boys see only architecture.
    • tat-daṁṣṭrābhiḥ — by his fangs
      Compound: tat (its) + daṁṣṭra (fang). The same word used in BG 11.27 when Arjuna sees the warriors crushed in the Lord's terrifying universal form. Here the daṁṣṭras are the demon's — and they will be the ones that fail.
    On the left and right, the two depressions resembling mountain caves are the corners of its mouth, and the high mountain peaks are its teeth.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    On the left and right, the two depressions resembling mountain caves are the corners of its mouth, and the high mountain peaks are its teeth.
    Commentary

    "Look here, these two caves (etah) appear to be like the front part of a mouth. And these mountains (pointing with his forefinger) (etah) appear like a serpent’s teeth."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Those groups of high mountain peaks which are right in front of us (etāḥ) compete with a snake’s teeth.

    Related values & verses
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture children pointing at a row of stalactites: 'Look, peaks of a mountain!' — except the peaks are teeth and the cave is a throat. Their cheerfulness in catalog-mode is itself a protective force. In Vraja, observing the danger calmly turns out to be safer than fleeing it.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.22

    ఆస్తృతాయామమార్గోఽయం రసనాం ప్రతిగర్జతి । ఏషామన్తర్గతం ధ్వాన్తమేతదప్యన్తరాననమ్ ॥ ౨౨ ॥
    āstṛtāyāma-mārgo ’yaṁ
    [aastritaayaama-maargo ’yam]
    rasanāṁ pratigarjati

    Important Words
    • āstṛta-āyāma — broad and extended
      Compound: āstṛta (spread out) + āyāma (length). The tongue is described as if it were architecture — a paved road. Bhāgavatam's interest in built scale recurs throughout (Hari's universal form, the demon's body, the Govardhana hill).
    • rasanām — his tongue
      Tongue, but also taste (rasa). A pun — the demon's rasanā is part of how he plans to taste the boys; their rasa is to laugh and walk in. Same word, opposite directions.
    • antaḥ-gatam — gone inside
      Compound: antaḥ (inside) + gata (gone). 'What has gone inside' — they are speaking already from inside the mouth. The verb form puts them in the demon's interior even as they discuss its anatomy.
    • antaḥ-ānanam — the inside of his mouth
      Compound: antaḥ (inside) + ānana (mouth). 'Inside-mouth' as a single concept — the cavity. Where the Aghāsura-līlā literally takes place; chapter 14 will reveal a deeper antar-ānana (the universal form inside Kṛṣṇa's mouth).
    In length and breadth the animal’s tongue resembles a broad traffic-way, and the inside of its mouth is very, very dark, like a cave in a mountain.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    In length and breadth the animal's tongue resembles a broad traffic-way, and the inside of its mouth is very, very dark, like a cave in a mountain.
    Commentary

    "This wide (astrtayamah) road is like a tongue (rasana). The darkness between the mountain peaks (esam) is like the opening of his mouth."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The long, wide (āsṛta) road resembles a tongue. The shadows of the cave are compared to the inside of a snake’s mouth because both were black.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    In Vṛndāvana, because of unlimited cows wandering about, it appeared there was a wide road naturally everywhere.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Bhakti (Devotion)Prema (Divine Love)
    Concepts
    RasaJiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a public square paved smooth so that footsteps echo — and the boys hear their own voices coming back to them inside the demon's mouth. The echo is the warning; their interpretation is 'oh, the road is paved.' Vraja's reading is consistently the rasika reading: every threat is geography, every roar is hospitality.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.23

    దావోష్ణఖరవాతోఽయం శ్వాసవద్భ‍ాతి పశ్యత । తద్దగ్ధసత్త్వదుర్గన్ధోఽప్యన్తరామిషగన్ధవత్ ॥ ౨౩ ॥

    Important Words
    • dāva-uṣṇa-khara-vātaḥ — a hot, harsh wind like a forest fire
      Compound: dāva (forest fire) + uṣṇa (hot) + khara (harsh) + vāta (wind). Four-stem compound — Bhāgavatam's poetic density. The image of dāvāgni recurs across Canto 10: chapter 17, chapter 19. Vraja survives every fire because Kṛṣṇa contains it.
    • tat-dagdha-sattva — the beings he has burned/eaten
      Compound: tat (his) + dagdha (burned) + sattva (creature). The bodies inside him are 'burned beings.' Gauḍīyas note: souls eaten by Aghāsura cease being mere food when Kṛṣṇa enters — chapter 38 confirms even the demon himself attains sārūpya.
    • durgandhaḥ — bad smell
      Compound: dur (bad) + gandha (smell). The boys observe the foul smell and identify it correctly. In Vraja, even noticing the rotten is part of the rasa — the children are precise observers, not idealists.
    The hot fiery wind is the breath coming out of his mouth, which is giving off the bad smell of burning flesh because of all the dead bodies he has eaten.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The hot fiery wind is the breath coming out of his mouth, which is giving off the bad smell of burning flesh because of all the dead bodies he has eaten.
    Commentary

    "The smell of animals burned in the forest fire seems to be like the smell of flesh digesting in a snake’s stomach."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    They speak in astonishment. Look! The hot wind resembles a snake’s breathing.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Imagine children noticing that a cave smells of cooked flesh and remarking calmly, 'must be old kills.' Their composure is not bravado — it is the natural fearlessness of those who have never been alone. The hot wind of Aghāsura's breath is the smell of every soul he has eaten; the boys walk through it as through a kitchen fire that cannot burn them.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.24

    ↗
    అస్మాన్ కిమత్ర గ్రసితా నివిష్టా- నయం తథా చేద్ బకవద్ వినఙ్‌క్ష్యతి । క్షణాదనేనేతి బకార్యుశన్ముఖం వీక్ష్యోద్ధసన్త: కరతాడనైర్యయు: ॥ ౨౪ ॥
    asmān kim atra grasitā niviṣṭān

    Important Words
    • baka-vat — like Baka
      baka (heron-demon) + -vat (like). The boys explicitly compare Aghāsura to Bakāsura of chapter 11. Gauḍīyas: the cowherd boys have already metabolized Kṛṣṇa's previous līlās — Vraja's children are also Bhāgavatam-students.
    • baka-ari-uśat-mukham — the beautiful face of the enemy of Baka
      Compound: baka (heron-demon) + ari (enemy) + uśat (beautiful) + mukha (face). Kṛṣṇa is named here by His relation to the previous demon — 'Baka-enemy.' Each pastime adds a name; Kṛṣṇa's name-store grows by demon-defeat.
    • uddhasantaḥ — laughing loudly
      ud + has- (to laugh out). The boys laugh as they enter the demon's mouth. Gauḍīyas read this as the perfect sakhya-fearlessness: the joke is on the demon, not on them.
    • kara-tāḍanaiḥ — by clapping hands
      Compound: kara (hand) + tāḍana (striking). Hand-clapping — the universal child's signal for game-on. The cowherd boys are playing kabaddi with a python; the rules are their own.
    Then the boys said, “Has this living creature come to swallow us? If he does so, he will immediately be killed like Bakāsura, without delay.” Thus they looked at the beautiful face of Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of Bakāsura, and, laughing loudly and clapping their hands, they entered the mouth of the python.
    Purport

    After talking about the terrible animal this way and that way, they decided to enter the demon’s mouth. They had full faith in Kṛṣṇa because they had experienced how Kṛṣṇa had saved them from the mouth of Bakāsura. Now, here was another asura, Aghāsura. Therefore, they wanted to enjoy the sport of entering the demon’s mouth and being saved by Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of Bakāsura.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Then the boys said, "Has this living creature come to swallow us? If he does so, he will immediately be killed like Bakāsura, without delay." Thus they looked at the beautiful face of Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of Bakāsura, and, laughing loudly and clapping their hands, they entered the mouth of the python.
    Commentary

    Together they spoke with a little fear. "This thing, if this is really a snake, and we enter his mouth, will he swallow us up?" Someone then answered to calm them down. "If it is so, then in an instant (ksanena) he will be killed as Baka was killed by Krsna (anena)." Saying this he smiled while looking at Krsna, who was standing some distance away. "Krsna is within our glance, so what have we to worry?" Getting this answer, they all began to laugh loudly and said, "Friends, we must find out what is in that mouth." Then they entered the mouth out of boyish curiosity. They ran in, clapping their hands, to show their fearlessness and courage, or to dismiss the snake. The calves also raising their tails ran after them inside the snake’s mouth.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Saying this, they laughed loudly on making analogies with a snake. Or they looked at Kṛṣṇa’s face and laughed loudly. Or they laughed to show that what they said was a joke, saying “You should be careful here.” To show courage and pride along with fearlessness, as if driving away a snake, they ran forward.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-12-24 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture children walking into a tiger's open mouth while pointing at a friend and saying, 'If it tries anything, he'll handle it.' That friend is Kṛṣṇa. Their entire calculation rests on Him; they do not even need to articulate it. Sakhya-rasa is the rasa in which faith does not look like faith — it looks like assumption.

    Stories
    Bakāsura swallowed and split

    Just one chapter earlier (SB 10.11), Bakāsura — Aghāsura's elder brother — appeared as a giant heron and swallowed Kṛṣṇa whole. Kṛṣṇa heated Himself inside the heron's throat until the demon released Him; then He grabbed the beak and tore the demon in two.

    The boys remember this. When verse 24 says 'he will be killed like Bakāsura,' they are not speculating — they are citing yesterday's precedent. Sakhya-rasa has memory; Kṛṣṇa's exploits are oral tradition among His friends.

    📝 My note

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.25

    ↗
    ఇత్థం మిథోఽతథ్యమతజ్జ్ఞభాషితం శ్రుత్వా విచిన్త్యేత్యమృషా మృషాయతే । రక్షో విదిత్వాఖిలభూతహృత్స్థిత: స్వానాం నిరోద్ధుం భగవాన్ మనో దధే ॥ ౨౫ ॥
    itthaṁ mitho ’tathyam ataj-jña-bhāṣitaṁ
    [ittham mitho ’tathyam ataj-jna-bhaashitam]
    śrutvā vicintyety amṛṣā mṛṣāyate
    [shrutvaa vichintyety amrishaa mrishaayate]

    [raksho viditvaakhila-bhoota-hrit-sthitah]

    Important Words
    • akhila-bhūta-hṛt-sthitaḥ — situated in the heart of all beings
      Compound: akhila (all) + bhūta (being) + hṛt (heart) + sthita (situated). The technical Paramātmā-vāda formulation. Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa is simultaneously the boy walking among them AND the antaryāmī inside everyone — acintya-bhedābheda live in one verse.
    • svānām — of His own
      From sva (one's own). The cowherd boys are sva — Kṛṣṇa's own. Possessive in the rasa-sense: relationship of belonging. Gauḍīyas insist on this — devotees are not 'others' to Kṛṣṇa but svajana, His own people.
    • niroddhum — to stop
      From ni + rudh- (to restrain). Kṛṣṇa wants to restrain His friends — protective instinct. Significantly, the verse ends with niroddhum manaḥ dadhe ('He set His mind to restrain'); but in verse 26 they enter anyway, faster than His thought.
    • bhagavān — the Supreme Personality of Godhead
      The technical full title. Inserted at the moment Kṛṣṇa decides to act as Lord, not just as friend. Gauḍīyas distinguish bhagavattā (His Lordship) and mādhurya (His sweetness); both are in Him simultaneously, and Bhāgavatam toggles vocabulary as the situation requires.
    The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is situated as antaryāmī, the Supersoul, in the core of everyone’s heart, heard the boys talking among themselves about the artificial python. Unknown to them, it was actually Aghāsura, a demon who had appeared as a python. Kṛṣṇa, knowing this, wanted to forbid His associates to enter the demon’s mouth.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is situated as antaryāmī, the Supersoul, in the core of everyone's heart, heard the boys talking among themselves about the artificial python. Unknown to them, it was actually Aghāsura, a demon who had appeared as a python. Kṛṣṇa, knowing this, wanted to forbid His associates to enter the demon's mouth.
    Commentary

    Hearing the boys discuss about the real snake as if it were something else Krsna began to think. "What is really a snake’s mouth they are thinking is some scenic part of Vrndavan, not a snake’s mouth. This is not just a snake, but the demon called Aghasura. " How did he know this? Because he is situated in everyone’s heart (akhila bhuta hrt sthitah) as paramatma, knowing everything. He then decided to prevent them (svanam) from entering.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Hearing the speech of his friends, who were acting like ignorant people, hearing that speech which was false, since truly (amṛṣā) the gaping mouth of the snake shone with brilliance (mṛṣāyate) in Vṛndāvana, and knowing it was not only a python but the Rākṣasa named Agha, since he was in everyone’s heart as Paramātmā, was the inspiration for all things and was endowed with all powers (bhagavān), he wanted to prevent them.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a father who hears his children, several rooms away, discussing whether the trapdoor is real — and rises to intervene. Kṛṣṇa as antaryāmī is not far in the heart; He is the listener of every word, including the ones spoken by His friends about a danger they cannot see clearly. The verse marks the moment Vraja-līlā tips: until now Kṛṣṇa was a peer, here He is also the Lord considering His own role.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.26

    ↗
    తావత్ ప్రవిష్టాస్త్వసురోదరాన్తరం పరం న గీర్ణా: శిశవ: సవత్సా: । ప్రతీక్షమాణేన బకారివేశనం హతస్వకాన్తస్మరణేన రక్షసా ॥ ౨౬ ॥
    tāvat praviṣṭās tv asurodarāntaraṁ
    [taavat pravishtaas tv asurodaraantaram]
    paraṁ na gīrṇāḥ śiśavaḥ sa-vatsāḥ
    [param na geernaah shishavah sa-vatsaah]
    pratīkṣamāṇena bakāri-veśanaṁ
    [prateekshamaanena bakaari-veshanam]
    hata-sva-kānta-smaraṇena rakṣasā
    [hata-sva-kaanta-smaranena rakshasaa]
    Word-for-word
    tāvat — in the meantime praviṣṭāḥ — all entered tu — indeed asura - udara - antaram — within the belly of the great demon param — but na gīrṇāḥ — they were not swallowed śiśavaḥ — all the boys sa - vatsāḥ — along with their calves pratīkṣamāṇena — who was just waiting for baka - ari — of the enemy of Bakāsura veśanam — the entering hata - sva - kānta - smaraṇena — the asura was thinking of his own dead relatives, who would not be satisfied unless Kṛṣṇa were dead rakṣasā — by the demon .

    rakṣasā
    by the demon .
    Important Words
    • asura-udara-antaram — into the belly of the demon
      Compound: asura (demon) + udara (belly) + antara (inside). The same udara-interior that the universal form contains all worlds in (chapter 14) here contains the cowherd boys. The līlā creates a deliberate inversion: in the demon's belly there is loss; in Kṛṣṇa's belly there is everything.
    • śiśavaḥ — the children
      Plural of śiśu (child). Gauḍīyas read śiśu as the keyword of kaumāra-līlā — Kṛṣṇa as śiśu is the form Yaśodā holds, the gopas play with. Mādhurya-rasa belongs to later years; here we are still in śiśu-rasa, where vātsalya and sakhya predominate.
    • baka-ari — the enemy of Baka (i.e. Kṛṣṇa)
      Compound: baka + ari (enemy). The demon's own internal monologue names Kṛṣṇa by His Baka-killer title. Even Aghāsura's mind is full of Kṛṣṇa's exploits — Vraja's enemies are devotional by inversion.
    • hata-sva-kānta-smaraṇena — by remembrance of his slain kin
      Compound: hata (slain) + sva (own) + kānta (dear one) + smaraṇa (remembrance). The demon's smaraṇa is grief-driven memory of his siblings. Gauḍīyas note: smaraṇa is a limb of bhakti (śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ smaraṇam); the demon's smaraṇa, though hostile, still keeps Kṛṣṇa in mind — which is why even hostile thought leads to liberation (verse 39).
    In the meantime, while Kṛṣṇa was considering how to stop them, all the cowherd boys entered the mouth of the demon. The demon, however, did not swallow them, for he was thinking of his own relatives who had been killed by Kṛṣṇa and was just waiting for Kṛṣṇa to enter his mouth.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    In the meantime, while Kṛṣṇa was considering how to stop them, all the cowherd boys entered the mouth of the demon. The demon, however, did not swallow them, for he was thinking of his own relatives who had been killed by Kṛṣṇa and was just waiting for Kṛṣṇa to enter his mouth.
    Commentary

    Just as Krsna decided to stop them, the boys entered the serpent’s mouth, but the serpent did not swallow them. Why didn’t he swallow them? Remembering the death of his brother and sister (hata svaka antasmaranena) he was waiting for Krsna. To think that though Krsna wanted to stop the boys from entering he could not do it, and that his satyasankalpa sakti(every desire of the Lord is fulfilled) was thwarted, is incorrect. The devotees had the desire , " If we go in the serpent’s mouth, will he swallow us? If he swallows us, our friend will kill him as he did Baka." Of the two desires, the desire of the devotee prevails, as it was explained previously, by the principle that the Lord is controlled by his devotee. This is all arranged by the ever- watchful lila sakti of the Lord, which destroys all contradictions.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Just as he decided to stop them, the boys along with the calves entered the snake’s mouth. This incident, like Kṛṣṇa’s external pastimes of killing demons, takes place because his dear devotees were absorbed in prema-rasa. This is the real conclusion as with previous incidents. Otherwise this could not happen, since Kṛṣṇa’s every wish is fulfilled (satya-saṅkalpa) and he is the very form of all knowledge. The calves entered because the boys followed after them, clapping their hands and making them run, since otherwise they would have fled away in fear. The boys with the calves went into the stomach of Agha, though they did not at all want to enter, since it frightfully dark and full of fire. Though they were unlimited in number, by the influence of the līlā-śakti, they could all fit inside. Agha did not prevent them from leaving by closing his mouth and swallowing (gīrṇāḥ). Another version has na jīrṇāḥ: he did not want to digest them. The meaning becomes the same since closing the mouth and stretching the body are aids to digesting food.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Just as he decided to stop them, the boys along with the calves entered the snake’s mouth. This incident, like Kṛṣṇa’s external pastimes of killing demons, takes place because his dear devotees were absorbed in prema-rasa. This is the real conclusion as with previous incidents. Otherwise this could not happen, since Kṛṣṇa’s every wish is fulfilled (satya-saṅkalpa) and he is the very form of all knowledge. One should not say that that his external actions, which are for the benefit of the world can be understood and the internal actions, of he who is deeper than an million oceans, cannot be understood, for he has nothing to hide from his dear devotees. Ullaṅghita-trividha-sīma-samātiśāyi saṁbhāvanaṁ tava parivraḍima-svabhāvam Māyā-balena bhavatāpi niguhymānaṁ paśyanti kecid aniśaṁ tvad ananya-bhāvāḥ O Lord who surpasses three types of limitations! Some persons, having pure devotion to you, constantly see your fame and generous nature, though it is hidden by you by the power of your māyā. Stotra-ratna, Yamunācārya If Kṛṣṇa’s thoughts were beyond comprehension, Śukadeva would not be able to describe his thinking in verse 25 (vicintya). Or while Kṛṣṇa decided to prevent the boys from entering, before that (tāvat) before his desire, they entered. Or hearing the talk of the boys (svānām) he decided to kill (nirodhum) the demon (rakṣaḥ). This resolves the issue. The demon did not close his mouth and swallow the boys. He was waiting for Kṛṣṇa.

    Concepts
    MayaSatya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a host waiting for one specific guest to arrive before serving — refusing to swallow the friends because the real prize is the boy at their center. Aghāsura's restraint is itself his miscalculation: he could have taken what he had; instead he held out for what he could not have.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.27

    ↗
    తాన్ వీక్ష్య కృష్ణ: సకలాభయప్రదో హ్యనన్యనాథాన్ స్వకరాదవచ్యుతాన్ । దీనాంశ్చ మృత్యోర్జఠరాగ్నిఘాసాన్ ఘృణార్దితో దిష్టకృతేన విస్మిత: ॥ ౨౭ ॥
    tān vīkṣya kṛṣṇaḥ sakalābhaya-prado
    [taan veekshya krishnah sakalaabhaya-prado]
    hy ananya-nāthān sva-karād avacyutān
    [hy ananya-naathaan sva-karaad avachyutaan]
    dīnāṁś ca mṛtyor jaṭharāgni-ghāsān
    [deenaamsh cha mrityor jatharaagni-ghaasaan]
    ghṛṇārdito diṣṭa-kṛtena vismitaḥ
    [ghrinaardito dishta-kritena vismitah]
    Word-for-word
    tān — all those boys vīkṣya — seeing kṛṣṇaḥ — the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa sakala - abhaya - pradaḥ — who is the source of fearlessness for everyone hi — indeed ananya - nāthān — especially for the cowherd boys, who did not know anyone except Kṛṣṇa sva - karāt — from the control of His hand avacyutān — now gone out dīnān ca — helpless mṛtyoḥ jaṭhara - agni - ghāsān — who had all entered like straws into the fire of the abdomen of Aghāsura, who was very bold and hungry, like death personified (because the asura had assumed a big body, he must have had a very strong appetite) ghṛṇā - arditaḥ — therefore, being compassionate due to causeless mercy diṣṭa - kṛtena — by things arranged by His internal potency vismitaḥ — He also, for the time being, was astonished .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)
    tān vīkṣya kṛṣṇaḥ sakalābhaya-prado
    tān
    all those boys
    vīkṣya
    seeing
    kṛṣṇaḥ
    the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa
    sakala - abhaya - pradaḥ
    who is the source of fearlessness for everyone
    hi
    indeed
    hy ananya-nāthān sva-karād avacyutān
    ananya - nāthān
    especially for the cowherd boys, who did not know anyone except Kṛṣṇa
    sva - karāt
    from the control of His hand
    avacyutān
    now gone out
    dīnāṁś ca mṛtyor jaṭharāgni-ghāsān
    dīnān ca
    helpless
    mṛtyoḥ jaṭhara - agni - ghāsān
    who had all entered like straws into the fire of the abdomen of Aghāsura, who was very bold and hungry, like death personified (because the asura had assumed a big body, he must have had a very strong appetite)
    ghṛṇā - arditaḥ
    therefore, being compassionate due to causeless mercy
    ghṛṇārdito diṣṭa-kṛtena vismitaḥ
    diṣṭa - kṛtena
    by things arranged by His internal potency
    vismitaḥ
    He also, for the time being, was astonished .
    Important Words
    • sakala-abhaya-pradaḥ — the giver of all fearlessness
      Compound: sakala (all) + abhaya (fearlessness) + prada (giver). Kṛṣṇa's standing title — abhaya-mudrā Lord. Gauḍīyas: His very nature is to remove fear, even when momentarily He shows the appearance of considering. The title in this verse is the answer to the verse's question.
    • ananya-nāthān — having no other Lord
      Compound: ananya (no other) + nātha (Lord). The boys recognize no Lord but Kṛṣṇa. This is the technical definition of pure bhakti — ananya-bhakti, exclusive devotion. Gauḍīyas (Rūpa Gosvāmī, Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.1.11) make this exclusivity the very mark of uttamā bhakti.
    • sva-karāt avacyutān — slipped from His own hand
      Compound: sva (own) + kara (hand) + avacyuta (fallen, slipped). The friends have slipped from His grasp — a maximally tender image. Even the Lord's hand can momentarily not hold what walks past it; this is the līlā's pretense of vulnerability.
    • diṣṭa-kṛtena — as if arranged by providence
      Compound: diṣṭa (destined) + kṛta (done). 'As if done by fate' — a curious phrase, since Kṛṣṇa is fate. Gauḍīyas read this as Bhagavān playing inside His own internal energy (yoga-māyā), letting the situation unfold.
    Kṛṣṇa saw that all the cowherd boys, who did not know anyone but Him as their Lord, had now gone out of His hand and were helpless, having entered like straws into the fire of the abdomen of Aghāsura, who was death personified. It was intolerable for Kṛṣṇa to be separated from His friends the cowherd boys. Therefore, as if seeing that this had been arranged by His internal potency, Kṛṣṇa was momentarily struck with wonder and unsure of what to do.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Kṛṣṇa saw that all the cowherd boys, who did not know anyone but Him as their Lord, had now gone out of His hand and were helpless, having entered like straws into the fire of the abdomen of Aghāsura, who was death personified. It was intolerable for Kṛṣṇa to be separated from His friends the cowherd boys. Therefore, as if seeing that this had been arranged by His internal potency, Kṛṣṇa was momentarily struck with wonder and unsure of what to do.
    Commentary

    Seeing the boys escape from his hands like jewels and fall into the fire of the snake’s stomach like blades of grass, moved with compassion (ghrnaya arditah), Krsna was astonished at the actions of his lila sakti (dista krtena) or of time (dista) in having them enter the serpent’s mouth. What was the cause of astonishment? "It is not possible for these cowherd boys to have prarabdha karma and besides, the supersoul does not give them the tendency for karma. Because supersoul is my expansion, she does not do anything unfavorable to me. Therefore the lila sakti has made this seemingly unfavorable arrangement with a desire to put me, full of prema, into the compassionate mood (karuna rasa) on seeing this unfortunate situation. How astonishing is her control over me, the form of all rasas, the king of all lila ( lila purusottama)." Though the sorrow due to separation from his friends remained, with the rise of astonishment (vismaya bhava), it reduced a little. This is the meaning.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Kṛṣṇa who is the giver of fearlessness to the universe saw that the boys solely depending on him had gone far away from his hands which give fearlessness. They were now suffering since by dying they would be separated from him and were now like grass in the fire of digestion of Agha, who was death personified. He became astonished by the results given through prārabdha-karma (diṣta-kṛteṇa). But actually this event occurred according to the mood of the Lord who is filled with pleasure by performing human-like pastimes. Diṣṭa means his appointed (diṣṭa) śakti which covers his pastimes when the Lord comes as avatāra. Or diṣṭa means a principal following his approval. Pāṇini 4.4.60 says asti nasty diṣṭaṁ matiḥ: the suffix ka is added to diṣṭa to indicate a believer in things based on proof. He was astonished by what had been done before his eyes (diṣṭa-kṛteṇa). He was amazed that in front of him, his dear friends had been swallowed up. Or vismitaḥ can mean that he lost his smile out of grief. “Even if Kṛṣṇa was filled with grief, how were the boys under control of karma?” His discrimination vanished because of being controlled by affection (gṛṇārditaḥ). His nature is to be most merciful. Or, though he was soft with compassion for them because they had no other shelter (ananya-nāthān), he understood that they had escaped from his hands and were about to die. Therefore he was astonished.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a parent who turns for one moment and finds the children have all walked into the river — and the parent, who is also the river's master, stands stunned for an instant. Kṛṣṇa's pause is not weakness; it is the līlā's deliberate beat. The Supreme Person allows Himself to feel for a moment what every devotee has felt: that the beloved is in danger and the path forward is not yet clear.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.28

    ↗
    కృత్యం కిమత్రాస్య ఖలస్య జీవనం న వా అమీషాం చ సతాం విహింసనమ్ । ద్వయం కథం స్యాదితి సంవిచిన్త్య జ్ఞాత్వావిశత్తుణ్డమశేషద‍ృగ్ఘరి: ॥ ౨౮ ॥
    kṛtyaṁ kim atrāsya khalasya jīvanaṁ
    [krityam kim atraasya khalasya jeevanam]
    na vā amīṣāṁ ca satāṁ vihiṁsanam
    [na vaa ameeshaam cha sataam vihimsanam]
    dvayaṁ kathaṁ syād iti saṁvicintya
    [dvayam katham syaad iti samvichintya]
    jñātvāviśat tuṇḍam aśeṣa-dṛg ghariḥ
    [jnaatvaavishat tundam ashesha-drig gharih]
    Word-for-word
    kṛtyam kim — what to do atra — in this situation asya khalasya — of this envious demon jīvanam — the existence of life na — there should not be vā — either amīṣām ca — and of those who are innocent satām — of the devotees vihiṁsanam — the death dvayam — both actions (killing the demon and saving the boys) katham — how syāt — can be possible iti saṁvicintya — very perfectly thinking about the subject matter jñātvā — and deciding what to do aviśat — entered tuṇḍam — within the mouth of the demon aśeṣa - dṛk hariḥ — Kṛṣṇa, who has unlimited potency, could understand past, future and present .

    Kṛṣṇa, who has unlimited potency, could understand past, future and present .
    Important Words
    • kṛtyam — what is to be done
      From kṛ- (to do). The technical word for required action — duty, the right next move. Kṛṣṇa is shown deliberating like a competent agent. Gauḍīyas read this as līlā-deliberation; the Lord 'considers' though He knows.
    • khalasya jīvanam — the life of the wicked one
      Compound: khala (wicked) + jīvana (life). Aghāsura's death is named as the first half of the problem. Bhāgavatam is unflinching: killing a demon is a legitimate divine action.
    • satām vihiṁsanam — harm to the devotees
      Compound: sat (devotee, truth-keeper) + vihiṁsana (harm). The second half. Kṛṣṇa names the protection of the devotees in the same breath as the killing of the demon — both are equally divine work.
    • aviśat tuṇḍam — He entered the mouth
      Compound verb: aviśat (entered) + tuṇḍam (mouth). The pivot of the chapter. Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān's entering the mouth of His own destroyer is the structural pattern of avatāra-līlā — the Lord walks into the very throat of evil to undo it from inside.
    Now, what was to be done? How could both the killing of this demon and the saving of the devotees be performed simultaneously? Kṛṣṇa, being unlimitedly potent, decided to wait for an intelligent means by which He could simultaneously save the boys and kill the demon. Then He entered the mouth of Aghāsura.
    Purport

    Kṛṣṇa is known as ananta-vīrya-sarvajña because everything is known to Him. Because He knows everything perfectly well, it was not difficult for Him to find a means by which He could save the boys and at the same time kill the demon. Thus He also decided to enter the demon’s mouth.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Now, what was to be done? How could both the killing of this demon and the saving of the devotees be performed simultaneously? Kṛṣṇa, being unlimitedly potent, decided to wait for an intelligent means by which He could simultaneously save the boys and kill the demon. Then He entered the mouth of Aghāsura.
    Commentary

    What should I do in this dangerous situation? How can I at once kill the demon without harming the boys? Thinking in this way, and finding a solution, he entered the mouth of the serpent, because he is one who knows what to do in the present and future (asesa drk) and he takes away (hari) danger (for the devotee) and life (for the demon).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    How to do both simultaneously? Thinking about this and understanding the solution, the attractive Lord who saw the perfect method entered the mouth of Aghāsura. Kṛṣṇa saw the perfect way (aśeṣa-dṛk) to enjoy pastimes and liberate the demon, to protect the devotees and destroy the rascals. Or he saw the unlimited calves and boys. He then entered the snake because he was overcome with affection by seeing their condition. He is called Hari because he would remove their suffering and attract their minds.

    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-12-28 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a master craftsman with two jobs that look mutually exclusive: save the friends already inside, and kill the killer who contains them. Lesser problem-solvers would sacrifice one for the other. Kṛṣṇa, being unlimited, waits for the cleaner solution — He enters the same mouth, and from inside accomplishes both. Bhakti's lesson: the Lord does not trade His devotees for His justice.

    Stories
    Prahlāda inside the fire

    Prahlāda Mahārāja (SB 7.5) was thrown into fire, poison, off cliffs, and into the coils of serpents — and each time emerged unharmed because Bhagavān protected him from inside whatever danger was meant to consume him. Hiraṇyakaśipu, like Aghāsura, found that the trap he set turned into the chamber of his own destruction.

    Gauḍīyas read both episodes through the same lens: Bhagavān saves the devotee not by removing the danger but by entering it. The cowherd boys in 10.12 walk into the python; the Person they belong to walks in after them and exits with them.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.29

    ↗
    తదా ఘనచ్ఛదా దేవా భయాద్ధాహేతి చుక్రుశు: । జహృషుర్యే చ కంసాద్యా: కౌణపాస్త్వఘబాన్ధవా: ॥ ౨౯ ॥
    tadā ghana-cchadā devā
    [tadaa ghana-chchadaa devaa]

    [bhayaad dhaa-heti chukrushuh]

    Important Words
    • ghana-chadāḥ — hidden behind clouds
      Compound: ghana (cloud) + chada (cover). The devas hide — they cannot bear to watch openly. Gauḍīyas: even demigods, with their long lives and powers, have only the courage to peek. The cowherd boys, by contrast, walked in laughing.
    • hā-hā — alas, alas
      Onomatopoeic vocative of grief. The same syllables that mark śoka in classical drama. Bhāgavatam uses everyday emotional sounds; the gods grieve in plain language.
    • kaṁsa-ādyāḥ — Kaṁsa and others
      Compound: Kaṁsa + ādi (etcetera). The demoniac faction. Gauḍīyas note: Kaṁsa's enmity is in fact constant smaraṇa — he thinks of Kṛṣṇa more than most devotees do. Vairānubandha is a form of bhakti in inversion, which is why Kaṁsa too is liberated by Kṛṣṇa's hand.
    • agha-bāndhavāḥ — friends of Agha
      Compound: agha (the demon, also 'sin') + bāndhava (kin, friend). 'Friends of sin' — a phrase that double-reads. Demoniac alliance is always alliance with sin itself. Gauḍīya satsaṅga logic: one is shaped by company.
    When Kṛṣṇa entered the mouth of Aghāsura, the demigods hidden behind the clouds exclaimed, “Alas! Alas!” But the friends of Aghāsura, like Kaṁsa and other demons, were jubilant.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    When Kṛṣṇa entered the mouth of Aghāsura, the demigods hidden behind the clouds exclaimed, "Alas! Alas!" But the friends of Aghāsura, like Kaṁsa and other demons, were jubilant.
    Commentary

    The devatas, who were hiding in the clouds out of fear of Kamsa, began shouting, fearing Krsna’s safety. Though the devatas are aware of the Lord’s powers, because they are also devotees, they shouted in anxiety. The very nature of bhakti is to have affection, and the very nature of affection is to lose ones logical discriminatory powers. Kamsa and the raksasas (kauna pa—eaters of corpses), the sons and brothers of Aghasura, rejoiced on hearing the news from messengers.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The devatās hid behind the cloud because of fear of Kaṁsa and Agha. The word ca indicates other sinful people as well. Though Kaṁsa was a daitya demon he was also a Rākṣasa. There are considered the same here to indicate their extremely evil nature. Or the sentence can indicate demons like Kaṁsa and Rākṣasa. They were all overjoyed since by nature they hated the Lord. Glee arose within their evil minds. They became joyful because Kaṁsa heard about Agha swallowing the boys and Kṛṣṇa through spies who had arrived from Vraja.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture two galleries watching the same act through opposite emotions: the demigods, who love Kṛṣṇa, cry 'alas, alas' from behind their clouds; Kaṁsa's people, who hate Him, cheer. The same event is heard in opposite registers depending on whether the heart belongs to Bhagavān. Bhakti is in part a question of which audience one is in.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.30

    ↗
    తచ్ఛ్రుత్వా భగవాన్కృష్ణస్త్వవ్యయ: సార్భవత్సకమ్ । చూర్ణీచికీర్షోరాత్మానం తరసా వవృధే గలే ॥ ౩౦ ॥
    tac chrutvā bhagavān kṛṣṇas
    [tach chrutvaa bhagavaan krishnas]
    tv avyayaḥ sārbha-vatsakam
    [tv avyayah saarbha-vatsakam]
    cūrṇī-cikīrṣor ātmānaṁ
    [choornee-chikeershor aatmaanam]
    tarasā vavṛdhe gale
    [tarasaa vavridhe gale]
    Word-for-word
    tat — that exclamation of hā-hā śrutvā — hearing bhagavān — the Supreme Personality of Godhead kṛṣṇaḥ — Lord Kṛṣṇa tu — indeed avyayaḥ — never vanquishable sa - arbha - vatsakam — along with the cowherd boys and the calves cūrṇī - cikīrṣoḥ — of that demon, who desired to smash within the abdomen ātmānam — personally, Himself tarasā — very soon vavṛdhe — enlarged gale — within the throat .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)
    tac chrutvā bhagavān kṛṣṇas

    that exclamation of hā-hā

    bhagavān
    the Supreme Personality of Godhead

    cūrṇī-cikīrṣor ātmānaṁ

    of that demon, who desired to smash within the abdomen
    ātmānam

    tarasā vavṛdhe gale
    tarasā
    very soon

    enlarged
    gale
    within the throat .
    Important Words
    • avyayaḥ — the inexhaustible one
      From a- (not) + vyaya (expenditure, decay). Kṛṣṇa is undiminishable. Bhagavad-gītā 2.17 uses the same word — 'know that to be indestructible by which all this is pervaded.' The Lord whom the demon thinks he is consuming is constitutively non-consumable.
    • sa-arbha-vatsakam — along with the boys and the calves
      Compound: sa (with) + arbha (boy) + vatsaka (little calf). Kṛṣṇa's protective concern includes the calves too. Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān's rakṣaṇa extends to every creature in His association, not just the human devotees.
    • cūrṇī-cikīrṣoḥ — of him who wished to grind them to powder
      Compound: cūrṇa (powder) + cikīrṣu (desirous to make). 'Powder-wanting-to-make' — a tight grammatical noun. The demon's intent named precisely; Kṛṣṇa's act is direct response.
    • vavṛdhe — He expanded
      From vṛdh- (to grow). Kṛṣṇa's body grew. Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān's body is acintya — limited and unlimited at once. The same hand Yaśodā holds in chapter 9 here fills the throat of an eight-mile demon. Acintya-bhedābheda of His own form.
    When the invincible Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, heard the demigods crying “Alas! Alas!” from behind the clouds, He immediately enlarged Himself within the demon’s throat, just to save Himself and the cowherd boys, His own associates, from the demon who wished to smash them.
    Purport

    Such are the acts of Kṛṣṇa. Paritrāṇāya sādhūnāṁ vināśāya ca duṣkṛtām ( Bg. 4.8 ). By enlarging Himself within the throat of the demon, Kṛṣṇa suffocated and killed him and at the same time saved Himself and His associates from imminent death and also saved the demigods from lamentation.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    When the invincible Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, heard the demigods crying "Alas! Alas!" from behind the clouds, He immediately enlarged Himself within the demon's throat, just to save Himself and the cowherd boys, His own associates, from the demon who wished to smash them.
    Commentary

    Hearing the sounds of dismay from the devatas, and the sounds of rejoicing from the demons, Krsna then expanded his form in the throat of the snake, who tried to swallow him into his stomach and digest him and the other boys. Krsna did this to reverse sounds of the lamentations of the devatas and the sounds of rejoicing of the demons. In the pastimes of killing Sakatasura, Trnavarta, and getting bound up, Krsna remained in a very small form and spread his powers. Therefore in the throat of Aghasura he expanded his form to show that he did not lack the power to expand himself in size. In the Damodara and other pastimes the Lord maintained his small form suitably because the devotees could taste in that form the full sweetness and within that, the astonishment caused by a show of power, with no disturbance to the conception of Krsna as their child. But here in the absence of the two parents who were relishing the sweetness, he, being the Lord, took the more common method of just expanding himself to kill the demon.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    When he heard the shouts of the devatās, Kṛṣṇa expanded himself in the throat of the demon who wanted to crush him and the boys by shutting his mouth and rolling his body about. He expanded his body to a big size, like a spike, in order to plug the throat of the demon so that his mouth would stay open and he would die. He could to this because he was Bhagavān, full of all powers and he did not decrease in power (avyayaḥ). Thus all fear of failure was removed. Or why did he expand himself? He does not allow dissipation of his devotees (avyayaḥ). He did it to protect his devotees. The reason is that he is Kṛṣṇa, whose heart is attracted (kṛṣ) by the devotees.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a flame that suddenly expands until the lantern it sits in cannot hold it — except here the lantern is a demon's throat, and the flame is the Lord of all worlds. Kṛṣṇa's response to His friends' danger is not a weapon and not a mantra; it is His own body, enlarged to choke evil from within.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.31

    ↗
    తతోఽతికాయస్య నిరుద్ధమార్గిణో హ్యుద్గీర్ణద‍ృష్టేర్భ్రమతస్త్వితస్తత: । పూర్ణోఽన్తరఙ్గే పవనో నిరుద్ధో మూర్ధన్ వినిర్భిద్య వినిర్గతో బహి: ॥ ౩౧ ॥
    tato ’tikāyasya niruddha-mārgiṇo
    [tato ’tikaayasya niruddha-maargino]
    hy udgīrṇa-dṛṣṭer bhramatas tv itas tataḥ
    [hy udgeerna-drishter bhramatas tv itas tatah]
    pūrṇo ’ntar-aṅge pavano niruddho
    [poorno ’ntar-ange pavano niruddho]
    mūrdhan vinirbhidya vinirgato bahiḥ
    [moordhan vinirbhidya vinirgato bahih]
    Word-for-word
    tataḥ — after Kṛṣṇa took action to kill the demon’s body from within the mouth ati - kāyasya — of that great demon, who had expanded his body to a very large size niruddha - mārgiṇaḥ — because of suffocating, all outlets being stopped up hi udgīrṇa - dṛṣṭeḥ — whose eyes had popped out bhramataḥ tu itaḥ tataḥ — the eyeballs, or the life air, moving here and there pūrṇaḥ — completely filled antaḥ - aṅge — within the body pavanaḥ — the life air niruddhaḥ — being stopped mūrdhan — the hole in the top of the head vinirbhidya — breaking vinirgataḥ — went out bahiḥ — externally .

    Important Words
    • ati-kāyasya — of him with an excessively large body
      Compound: ati (excessive) + kāya (body). The demon's body becomes hyperbolic — even bigger than at verse 16. Yet still finite. Gauḍīyas: no quantity of māyic expansion can match Bhagavān's qualitative infinity.
    • niruddha-mārgiṇaḥ — whose passages were blocked
      Compound: niruddha (blocked) + mārga (path). All openings sealed. The same niroddhum used in verse 25 (Kṛṣṇa wishing to restrain His friends) returns as niruddha — Kṛṣṇa restrains the demon instead. Verbal echo across the chapter.
    • mūrdhan vinirbhidya — breaking out through the head
      Compound: mūrdhan (crown of head) + vinirbhidya (breaking through). The prāṇa exits through the brahma-randhra — the same opening through which yogīs voluntarily leave the body at death (BG 8.10–13). Gauḍīyas read this as the gift of liberation given to Aghāsura through Kṛṣṇa's body.
    • pavanaḥ — life-air
      Wind, breath — but specifically the prāṇa. The demon's vital air, having nowhere else to go, departs as a liberated soul departs. The mechanism of his death is also the mechanism of his mukti.
    Then, because Kṛṣṇa had increased the size of His body, the demon extended his own body to a very large size. Nonetheless, his breathing stopped, he suffocated, and his eyes rolled here and there and popped out. The demon’s life air, however, could not pass through any outlet, and therefore it finally burst out through a hole in the top of the demon’s head.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Then, because Kṛṣṇa had increased the size of His body, the demon extended his own body to a very large size. Nonetheless, his breathing stopped, he suffocated, and his eyes rolled here and there and popped out. The demon's life air, however, could not pass through any outlet, and therefore it finally burst out through a hole in the top of the demon's head.
    Commentary

    His throat blocked up (niruddha marginoh), his eyes falling out, the air, trapped in his body with no way of getting out, went out through the brahma randhra at the top of the head, and attained perfection (purnah).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because Kṛṣṇa had expanded (tataḥ), Agha suffocated certainly (hi) and (tu) he became bewildered (bhramataḥ). He became like an insane person, thinking in pain “Where am I? Where will I go?” He became completely full, because his prāṇa was blocked. Thus his body swelled bigger and because of that, the prāṇa burst out of his head from a special spot (vi), the brahma-rundhra.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a balloon being inflated from the inside until it bursts — except the balloon is a demon's head, and the air is Kṛṣṇa Himself, growing without limit. The demon also tries to grow, but a finite creature cannot keep pace with the Infinite. His own life-air, with no other exit, breaks through the crown of his skull.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.32

    ↗
    తేనైవ సర్వేషు బహిర్గతేషు ప్రాణేషు వత్సాన్ సుహృద: పరేతాన్ । ద‍ృష్టయ‍ా స్వయోత్థాప్య తదన్విత: పున- ర్వక్త్రాన్ముకున్దో భగవాన్ వినిర్యయౌ ॥ ౩౨ ॥
    tenaiva sarveṣu bahir gateṣu

    Important Words
    • paretān — those who had passed away
      From parā + i (to go away). The boys and calves who had technically died inside the demon. Gauḍīyas: Bhagavān can revive what is already dead — He is mṛtyor mṛtyu, the death of death. Bhāgavatam 1.18 lists this as a sign of avatāra-tattva.
    • dṛṣṭyā svayā — by His own glance
      Compound: dṛṣṭi (glance) + sva (own). His glance alone revives them — no mantra, no implement. Gauḍīyas: Kṛṣṇa-darśana is itself life-giving; even a glance carries the full force of Bhagavān.
    • mukundaḥ — the giver of liberation
      muk (mukti) + da (giver). One of the Lord's principal names. Inserted at precisely the moment of giving life back — and also the moment of giving sārūpya-mukti to Aghāsura (verse 38). The name does double work: liberator of the boys, liberator of the demon.
    • viniryayau — He came out
      From vi + nir + yā (to go out completely). Kṛṣṇa walks out of the demon's mouth as casually as He walked in. Gauḍīyas: every entry of the Lord into apparent peril ends in viniryāṇa — going out unchanged, with all His own intact.
    When all the demon’s life air had passed away through that hole in the top of his head, Kṛṣṇa glanced over the dead calves and cowherd boys and brought them back to life. Then Mukunda, who can give one liberation, came out from the demon’s mouth with His friends and the calves.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    When all the demon's life air had passed away through that hole in the top of his head, Kṛṣṇa glanced over the dead calves and cowherd boys and brought them back to life. Then Mukunda, who can give one liberation, came out from the demon's mouth with His friends and the calves.
    Commentary

    The cowherd boys and calves who fainted due to separation from Krsna and from the heat of the serpent’s stomach, were brought back to consciousness by Krsna’s glance, which was a shower of nectar.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    When all his senses passed out along with the prāṇa (tena), or when all his life airs passed out through the brahma-randhra (tena), seeing the boys who momentarily looked as if dead because of following after the Lord’s human-like pastimes, he revived them to action by his sweet, affectionate glance. Mukunda, he who liberated Agha from saṁsāra and frees the boys from Agha, since he is the Supreme Lord who appeared on his own to benefit the universe (bhagavān), with the boys behind him (tad-anvitaḥ), came out of the snake’s mouth in bliss (vi=vinoda).

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Picture a master who steps out of a collapsed building with every child accounted for — and a few who had stopped breathing now revived by His glance. Mukunda walks out of Aghāsura's mouth as easily as out of a doorway, and behind Him come the friends He went in to save. The verse names Him Mukunda precisely here, where He gives mukti.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.12.33

    ↗
    పీనాహిభోగోత్థితమద్భ‍ుతం మహ- జ్జ్యోతి: స్వధామ్నా జ్వలయద్ దిశో దశ । ప్రతీక్ష్య ఖేఽవస్థితమీశనిర్గమం వివేశ తస్మిన్ మిషతాం దివౌకసామ్ ॥ ౩౩ ॥
    pīnāhi-bhogotthitam adbhutaṁ mahaj
    [peenaahi-bhogotthitam adbhutam mahaj]
    jyotiḥ sva-dhāmnā jvalayad diśo daśa
    [jyotih sva-dhaamnaa jvalayad disho dasha]
    pratīkṣya khe ’vasthitam īśa-nirgamaṁ
    [prateekshya khe ’vasthitam eesha-nirgamam]

    [vivesha tasmin mishataam divaukasaam]
